# ATL105 Test Data Validation – Consolidated Defect Report – Interlink Debit

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Product / program | BUYPASS ATL105 – Interlink Debit – Purchase/Capture, Authorization Only, Merchandise Return |
| Specification | BUYPASS Platform ATL105 Message Format Specifications **2026-4** (October 09, 2026) – `specifications/ATL105/docs/specs/BUYPASS_Platform_ATL105_Message_Format_Specifications_2026-4.pdf` |
| Test data under review | `interlink_debit/test_data` |
| Test case catalog | `interlink_debit/test_cases/test_cases.jsonl` (278 test cases in 21 scenarios) |
| Validator | `scripts/validate-td-against-atl105-spec.py` (field-by-field ATL105 rules; every quoted spec text re-verified on its page), run by `scripts/build-entry-mode-validation-report.py` |
| Scope | All 278 test cases of the catalog in 21 scenarios: 249 with test data (536 test data files, 63 of them Totals Requests) and 29 device-only test cases without a host message (ATL105 citations checked) |
| Date | 2026-11-04 |
| Overall result | **249 / 278 test cases FAIL** (29 REVIEW) – 13 defects: 9 systemic ([D-01](#d-01), [D-02](#d-02), [D-09](#d-09), [D-12](#d-12), [D-15](#d-15), [D-16](#d-16), [D-17](#d-17), [D-19](#d-19), [D-20](#d-20)), 4 scenario-specific ([D-14](#d-14), [D-18](#d-18), [D-21](#d-21), [D-24](#d-24)) |
| Purpose | Feedback for the AI test data generator team: for each defect the field, the ATL105 rule (verbatim), actual vs expected in every affected test data file, how to confirm it by hand and what to change |

Per-scenario detail: the findings reports listed in §8. Per-test-case detail: the `TC-xxxxxx_ATL105_Field_Validation.md` reports linked in every table. Test data links open the file at the line shown. Every finding is also in [Consolidated_Defect_Sheet.xlsx](./Consolidated_Defect_Sheet.xlsx) (one row per field finding, filterable).

Page numbers are PDF pages of the 2026-4 edition (printed page in brackets). Defect IDs are the same as in the scenario findings reports and the README.

---

## 1. What was validated

Every field of every test data file was checked against the ATL105 layouts and element definitions (details per scenario in §1 of each findings report):

| Area | Checks |
|---|---|
| Message / Data Section 1 | Message type per step, Element 55 `ATL105`, Element 63 = segments present |
| Segment 100 (17 fields) | Presence, character type, length and valid values; Prompt Code per step role; Segment Length recalculated from the fields plus Field Separators; run-time placeholders |
| Card data (Elements 2 + 12) | Track data, keyed (MMYY), stored credential and follow-on (YYMM) formats per entry mode; card-number check digit |
| Segment 111 | Tables 005 and 031, and the tables the transaction requires (030 for EMV and Credential on File, 032/049/056 for Credential on File, 003 for AVS) |
| Segment 130 | Elements 187–192 and 118, EMV chip data TLV, cross-field checks (9F02, 9F03, 9C, 5F34, 9F34, …) |
| Debit rules (Section 10.2) | Card type 011 in the Prompt Code; PIN block (Element 33) on every request except the Purchase Reversal and follow-on messages; no PIN block on follow-on messages; no manual (keyed) entry; no Time-out Reversal of a Completion |
| Totals Request (Segment 105) | Data Section 1 + Segment 105 only; every field (Prompt Code 990, Totals Date, versions, Card Label 4 characters, counts and amounts); Segment Length with 14 Field Separators; count, amount and grand total against the approved steps of the flow (Section 9.2) |
| Device-only test cases | No host message: the ATL105 text each test case quotes is checked on its page |
| Test objective | The data can prove what the test claims (expired card, online/offline PIN, Track 1, AVS, split tender, completion, cancellation, time-out reversal) |
| Step-to-step links | Sequence Number, Account Number, terminal, entry mode and amount against the original step |
| Test case catalog | Expected Response Codes, the test case's own `rule_checks` and `link_defs`, generation fidelity, ATL105 citations |

**How to read each defect section:** a summary table (field, where, type, how many files, actual, expected, ATL105 text, why, how the generator produced it, certainty, fix, check before release), then every affected field with actual vs expected, then numbered steps to confirm it by hand on one example.

Verdicts: **FAIL** violates ATL105 or the test case's own objective · **BLOCKED** holds instruction text that must be replaced by a run-time value · **REVIEW** cannot be decided from the spec or the test data alone (§6).

### Field-wise view

One row per ATL105 field: the defects found in it, an example of the generated value and what ATL105 expects.

| Field | Segment | Defect | Test cases | Files | Actual (example) | Expected (example) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Element 2 Account Number + Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (empty) | 100 | [D-15](#d-15) | 249 | 473 | `(empty)` (TC-002887) | Fill in the data read from the card (entry mode 05 = chip read) from the client's Interlink Debit test card. Until the card profile is supplied, keep the test case blocked and write an explicit placeholder instead of an empty field. |
| Element 33 Encrypted PIN Block Data (+ Tag 9F34) | 100 / 130 | [D-16](#d-16) | 242 | 305 | `(empty)` (TC-002887) | Element 33 = DUKPT Key Serial Number + PIN block from the client's test PIN and DUKPT key; until they are supplied, mark the test case blocked (needs client input). |
| Element 58 Nonfuel Amount (test case value) | 100 | [D-14](#d-14) | 7 | 7 | `37526954` (TC-002915) | Reversal amount = the step 1 response Approved Amount without the leading zeros (for example 000001000 -> 1000). |
| Element 84 Segment Length (Segment 100) | 100 | [D-14](#d-14) | 7 | 7 | `068` (TC-002915) | Recalculate the Segment Length after filling in the real values (for example Approved Amount 000001000 -> amount 1000 plus a 6-character Approval Number -> Segment Length 064). |
| Any field: test data vs test case value | 100 / 105 | [D-24](#d-24) | 14 | 14 | `029062` (TC-002906) | '029063' (regenerate the file from the test case) |
| Element 113 Table 005 (PAN entry mode) | 111 | [D-17](#d-17) | 34 | 43 | `791` (TC-003005) | Use a card-read entry mode – 80 (chip fallback to swipe) for the fallback scenario – or drop the keyed debit test case. |
| Element 113 Table 030 (POS Condition Code) | 111 | [D-12](#d-12) | 107 | 157 | `tables sent: 005, 031` (TC-002887) | Add Table 030 (10 digits, values from Appendix Q) for the certified terminal. |
| Element 113 Tables 032 / 049 / 056 (Credential on File) | 111 | [D-09](#d-09) | 35 | 67 | `tables sent: 005, 031` (TC-003035) | Add Table 032 sub-table 08 = 'C' (Credential on File); Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator 'C' or 'M'). |
| Element 118 EMV Additional Information | 130 | [D-02](#d-02) | 72 | 90 | `XTV641IPVR6UAAXEE82D...AYJLZN1D (984 characters)` (TC-002887) | EMVYES (device can receive EMV Table Data) or EMVNOT, with Element 192 = 006 (see the open question on framing); the Segment 130 length then becomes 0204 together with the Element 188 fix. |
| Element 188 EMV Card Sequence Number | 130 | [D-01](#d-01) | 72 | 90 | `01` (TC-002887) | '001' (then recalculate the Segment 130 length) |
| Element 190 EMV Chip Data – Tags 9F02 / 9F03 / 9C (cash back) | 130 | [D-18](#d-18) | 4 | 4 | `9F02=000000001500, 9F03=000000000000, 9C=00` (TC-002913) | Generate the chip data from a purchase with cash back: 9F02 = 000000002000, 9F03 = 000000000500, 9C = 09. |
| Element 13 Card Label (Totals Request) | 105 | [D-19](#d-19) | 63 | 63 | `'DB'` (TC-002893) | 'DB  ' (DB followed by 2 spaces) |
| Element 84 Segment Length (Totals Request) | 105 | [D-20](#d-20) | 63 | 63 | `108` (TC-002893) | 107 |
| Elements 16 / 15 / 42 Card Type Total Count, Amount, Grand Total | 105 | [D-21](#d-21) | 14 | 14 | `count 00001, amount 00000000, grand total 00000000` (TC-002896) | Leave out the DB bucket and send Grand Total 00000000 |

## 3. Systemic defects (one generator rule, every test data file of a kind)

<a id="d-01"></a>

### D-01 – EMV Card Sequence Number (Element 188) has the wrong width

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 188 EMV Card Sequence Number (Segment 130) |
| Where in the test data | `"EMV Request Data Segment"` → `"EMVCardSequenceNumber"` |
| Type | Wrong field width |
| Affected | 72 test cases, 90 test data files in 6 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS15 Merchandise Return – EMV Contact Chip; TS16 Merchandise Return – EMV Contactless Chip |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 90 of the 90 EMV test data files (Segment 130 sent) of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `01` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | '001' (then recalculate the Segment 130 length) |
| ATL105 reference | Element 188 EMV Card Sequence Number (Chapter 13), p.477 (printed 13-137): *"Fixed length of three bytes, right-aligned and padded with zeroes"*<br>Element 188 EMV Card Sequence Number (Chapter 13), p.477 (printed 13-137): *"Valid Codes/Values: 000—099 EMV Card Sequence Number is supplied by the EMV chip card"* |
| Why it is wrong | Element 188 is fixed at 3 bytes, right-aligned and zero-filled. The chip data carries Tag 5F34 = '01', so the card sequence number itself is right, but '01' is 2 bytes; it must be '001'. The declared Segment Length (1181) was calculated with the 2-byte value and changes by +1 when corrected. (TC-002887) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: realistic_emv_card_sequence` (90 of 90 files) – flags: `EMV Card Sequence Number = Tag 5F34 of the same card (element 188, p.270)` |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Element 188 = Tag 5F34 right-aligned and zero-padded to 3 digits (`01` → `001`); 3 spaces when Tag 5F34 is absent. Recalculate the Segment 130 Segment Length afterwards. |
| Check before release | Element 188 has 3 characters and equals Tag 5F34 zero-padded |

#### D-01 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002887, step 1)

1. Open [TD-TC-002887.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002887.json#L47) line 47: `EMVCardSequenceNumber` = `01` (2 characters).
2. In `EMVChipData` ([TD-TC-002887.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002887.json#L49) line 49) search for `5F34`: the next byte is the length (`01`) and the byte after it is the card sequence number → `01`.
3. Read in ATL105: Element 188 EMV Card Sequence Number (Chapter 13), p.477 (printed 13-137): *"Fixed length of three bytes, right-aligned and padded with zeroes"*; Element 188 EMV Card Sequence Number (Chapter 13), p.477 (printed 13-137): *"Valid Codes/Values: 000—099 EMV Card Sequence Number is supplied by the EMV chip card"*
4. Expected = Tag 5F34 right-aligned and zero-padded to 3 bytes = `001`; the file has `01` → **FAIL**.
5. Side check: Segment 130 `SegmentLength` = `1181` was counted with the 2-byte value and grows by 1 after the fix.
6. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

---

<a id="d-02"></a>

### D-02 – EMV Additional Information (Element 118) does not contain EMV Table Data

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 118 EMV Additional Information (with Elements 191/192) (Segment 130) |
| Where in the test data | `"EMV Request Data Segment"` → `"EMVAdditionalInformationIndicator"`, `"EMVAdditionalInformationLength"`, `"EMVAdditionalInformation"` |
| Type | Content not allowed for the indicator |
| Affected | 72 test cases, 90 test data files in 6 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS15 Merchandise Return – EMV Contact Chip; TS16 Merchandise Return – EMV Contactless Chip |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 90 of the 90 EMV test data files (Segment 130 sent) of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | 78 different values – see the table below (e.g. `XTV641IPVR6UAAXEE82D...AYJLZN1D (984 characters)`) |
| Expected (ATL105) | EMVYES (device can receive EMV Table Data) or EMVNOT, with Element 192 = 006 (see the open question on framing); the Segment 130 length then becomes 0204 together with the Element 188 fix. |
| ATL105 reference | Element 118 EMV Additional Information (Chapter 13), p.439 (printed 13-99): *"When EMV Additional Information Indicator has a value of 001, EMV table data is included here."*<br>Appendix T. EMV Additional Information Data Layouts - EMV Table Data, p.701 (printed Appendix T-1): *"EMVYES This code indicates that the device is able to receive EMV Table Data."*<br>Appendix T. EMV Additional Information Data Layouts - EMV Table Data, p.701 (printed Appendix T-1): *"EMVNOT This code indicates that the device is unable to receive EMV Table Data."*<br>Appendix T. EMV Additional Information Data Layouts - EMV Table Data, p.701 (printed Appendix T-1): *"Maximum Length n6 Len.: 6"* |
| Why it is wrong | Indicator 001 means EMV Table Data, and Appendix T allows only EMVYES or EMVNOT (6 characters). The field holds 984 generated characters. Element 192 = '984' matches that length, so it is consistent with the content, but the content itself is not allowed for indicator 001. (TC-002887) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: format_synthesis` (90 of 90 files) – flags: `UNTRUSTED_VALID_VALUES` |
| Certainty | Certain that the content is invalid; the exact corrected Element 192 value depends on the framing question in §6 |
| Fix in the generator | Indicator `001`: send EMV Table Data `EMVYES` (device can receive EMV table data) or `EMVNOT` and set Element 192 to its length. Apply the Appendix T values instead of filling the 984-character maximum, never reuse a generated string across test cases, and recalculate the Segment 130 length. |
| Check before release | Element 118 is `EMVYES` or `EMVNOT` (or the confirmed framed form) and Element 192 = its length |

#### D-02 – actual vs expected, field by field

Expected in every row: EMVYES (device can receive EMV Table Data) or EMVNOT, with Element 192 = 006 (see the open question on framing); the Segment 130 length then becomes 0204 together with the Element 188 fix.

… and 10 more rows of the same kind. Every row is in [Consolidated_Defect_Sheet.xlsx](./Consolidated_Defect_Sheet.xlsx), sheet *Field findings* (filter *Defect* = D-02).

#### D-02 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002887, step 1)

1. Open [TD-TC-002887.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002887.json#L50) line 50: indicator `001`, `EMVAdditionalInformationLength` = `984`, `EMVAdditionalInformation` = 984 characters (`XTV641IPVR6UAAXEE82D…AYJLZN1D`).
2. Indicator meaning: Element 191 EMV Additional Information Indicator (Chapter 13), p.479 (printed 13-139): *"001 EMV table data"*
3. Content for this indicator: Element 118 EMV Additional Information (Chapter 13), p.439 (printed 13-99): *"When EMV Additional Information Indicator has a value of 001, EMV table data is included here."*
4. Allowed values: Appendix T. EMV Additional Information Data Layouts - EMV Table Data, p.701 (printed Appendix T-1): *"EMVYES This code indicates that the device is able to receive EMV Table Data."*; Appendix T. EMV Additional Information Data Layouts - EMV Table Data, p.701 (printed Appendix T-1): *"EMVNOT This code indicates that the device is unable to receive EMV Table Data."*; Appendix T. EMV Additional Information Data Layouts - EMV Table Data, p.701 (printed Appendix T-1): *"Maximum Length n6 Len.: 6"*
5. The field holds 984 generated characters instead of `EMVYES` / `EMVNOT` → **FAIL**. Element 192 = `984` equals that length, so it is consistent with the wrong content and must change with the fix (framing: §6).
6. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

---

<a id="d-09"></a>

### D-09 – Credential on File indicators (Tables 032, 049, 056) are missing

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 113 Tables 032 / 049 / 056 (Credential on File indicators) (Segment 111) |
| Where in the test data | `"Variable Information Data Segment"` → `"VariableInformationSection"` |
| Type | Required data missing |
| Affected | 35 test cases, 67 test data files in 3 scenarios: TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS13 Authorization Only – Credential on File; TS20 Merchandise Return – Credential on File |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 67 of the 67 Credential on File test data files (entry mode 10, and the storing step of the T7 tests) of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `tables sent: 005, 031` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | Add Table 032 sub-table 08 = 'C' (Credential on File); Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator 'C' or 'M'). |
| ATL105 reference | Appendix I. Variable Information Data Layouts - POS Additional Information (Table 032), Sub Table 08, p.575 (printed Appendix I-26): *"It is merchant’s responsibility to send the indicator as Initial (“I”) or Subsequent Credential on File (“C”) from the terminal for all COF transactions (Authorization, Completion, Cancellation, Reversal etc.)."*<br>Appendix I. Variable Information Data Layouts - Merchant Supplementary Data (Table 049), Sub Table 02, p.589 (printed Appendix I-40): *"This field is required for all subsequent Credential on File transactions."*<br>Appendix J. Valid Point-of-Service Entry Mode Codes, p.638 (printed Appendix J-1): *"10 Credential on file"* |
| Why it is wrong | Table 005 entry mode 10 marks a transaction with stored credentials. ATL105 requires the merchant to send the Initial/Credential on File indicator for all COF transactions, the Transaction Initiation Indicator for all subsequent COF transactions. Missing: Table 032 sub-table 08 = 'C' (Credential on File); Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator 'C' or 'M'). (TC-003035) |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Credential on File: add Table 032 sub-table 08 (`C`; `I` on the transaction that stores the credential), Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator `C` or `M`) and, for MasterCard, Table 056 sub-table 09 (MIT/CIT category) on every COF step, including reversals and completions. |
| Check before release | every COF step has Tables 032-08 and 049-02 (and 056-09 for MasterCard); the storing step has 032-08 = `I` |

#### D-09 – actual vs expected, field by field

Actual in every row: `tables sent: 005, 031`
<br>Expected in every row: Add Table 032 sub-table 08 = 'C' (Credential on File); Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator 'C' or 'M').

#### D-09 – how to validate manually (example: TC-003035, step 1)

1. Open [TD-TC-003035.json](../test_data/TD-TC-003035.json#L30) line 30: Segment 111 lists Tables `005`, `031` (read each `VariableInformationIndicator`).
2. Table 005 entry mode `10` = credential on file.
3. Table 005 entry mode 10 marks a transaction with stored credentials. ATL105 requires the merchant to send the Initial/Credential on File indicator for all COF transactions, the Transaction Initiation Indicator for all subsequent COF transactions. Missing: Table 032 sub-table 08 = 'C' (Credential on File); Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator 'C' or 'M').
4. Read in ATL105: Appendix I. Variable Information Data Layouts - POS Additional Information (Table 032), Sub Table 08, p.575 (printed Appendix I-26): *"It is merchant’s responsibility to send the indicator as Initial (“I”) or Subsequent Credential on File (“C”) from the terminal for all COF transactions (Authorization, Completion, Cancellation, Reversal etc.)."*; Appendix I. Variable Information Data Layouts - Merchant Supplementary Data (Table 049), Sub Table 02, p.589 (printed Appendix I-40): *"This field is required for all subsequent Credential on File transactions."*; Appendix J. Valid Point-of-Service Entry Mode Codes, p.638 (printed Appendix J-1): *"10 Credential on file"*
5. Conclusion: **FAIL** – expected: Add Table 032 sub-table 08 = 'C' (Credential on File); Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator 'C' or 'M').
6. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

<a id="d-12"></a>

### D-12 – Table 030 (POS Condition Code) is missing on EMV and Credential on File transactions

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 113 Table 030 (POS Condition Code) (Segment 111) |
| Where in the test data | `"Variable Information Data Segment"` → `"VariableInformationSection"` |
| Type | Required data missing |
| Affected | 107 test cases, 157 test data files in 9 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS13 Authorization Only – Credential on File; TS15 Merchandise Return – EMV Contact Chip; TS16 Merchandise Return – EMV Contactless Chip; TS20 Merchandise Return – Credential on File |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 157 of the 157 EMV and Credential on File test data files of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `tables sent: 005, 031` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | Add Table 030 (10 digits, values from Appendix Q) for the certified terminal. |
| ATL105 reference | Appendix I. Variable Information Data Layouts - POS Condition Code (Table 030), p.568 (printed Appendix I-19): *"Required for all EMV transactions, all internet based transactions (Ex: Mobile Commerce, ecommerce and In App), and all Credential on File transactions."* |
| Why it is wrong | This step is an EMV transaction (Segment 130 is sent). Table 030 identifies the device class, presentation data, security condition and terminal type, and ATL105 requires it for all EMV and all Credential on File transactions. (TC-002887) |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Add Table 030 (POS Condition Code, 10 digits, values from Appendix Q for the certified terminal) to every EMV request and every Credential on File request. |
| Check before release | Segment 130 present or entry mode 10 → Table 030 present |

#### D-12 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002887, step 1)

1. Open [TD-TC-002887.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002887.json#L30) line 30: Segment 111 lists Tables `005`, `031` (read each `VariableInformationIndicator`).
2. Segment 130 is sent (EMV request); Table 005 entry mode `05` = chip read.
3. This step is an EMV transaction (Segment 130 is sent). Table 030 identifies the device class, presentation data, security condition and terminal type, and ATL105 requires it for all EMV and all Credential on File transactions.
4. Read in ATL105: Appendix I. Variable Information Data Layouts - POS Condition Code (Table 030), p.568 (printed Appendix I-19): *"Required for all EMV transactions, all internet based transactions (Ex: Mobile Commerce, ecommerce and In App), and all Credential on File transactions."*
5. Conclusion: **FAIL** – expected: Add Table 030 (10 digits, values from Appendix Q) for the certified terminal.
6. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

<a id="d-15"></a>

### D-15 – The card data (Account Number, Card Discretionary Block Data) is empty

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 2 Account Number and Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (Segment 100) |
| Where in the test data | `"Standard Message Data Segment"` → `"AccountNumber"`, `"CardDiscretionaryBlockData"` |
| Type | Required data missing (needs client input) |
| Affected | 249 test cases, 473 test data files in 21 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS3 Purchase/Capture – Magnetic Stripe; TS4 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS7 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS10 Authorization Only – Magnetic Stripe; TS11 Authorization Only – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS12 Authorization Only – Chip Fallback; TS13 Authorization Only – Credential on File; TS14 Authorization Only – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device; TS15 Merchandise Return – EMV Contact Chip; TS16 Merchandise Return – EMV Contactless Chip; TS17 Merchandise Return – Magnetic Stripe; TS18 Merchandise Return – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS19 Merchandise Return – Chip Fallback; TS20 Merchandise Return – Credential on File; TS21 Merchandise Return – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 473 of the 473 financial test data files (Segment 100 sent) of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `(empty)` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | per row in the table below (e.g. Fill in the data read from the card (entry mode 05 = chip read) from the client's Interlink Debit test card. Until the card profile is supplied, keep the test case blocked and write an explicit placeholder instead of an empty field.) |
| ATL105 reference | Element 2 Account Number (Chapter 13), p.364 (printed 13-24): *"a variable length (up to 24 alphanumeric bytes) Account Number"*<br>Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (Chapter 13), p.371 (printed 13-31): *"If the Account Number is obtained from a Track 2 card reader, instead of being manually keyed, the Account Number and Card Discretionary Block Data should consist of all data read from the card between, but not including, the Start Sentinel and End Sentinel."*<br>Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (Chapter 13), p.371 (printed 13-31): *"Account Number is considered the data before the = character, and Card Discretionary Block Data is considered the data after the = character."*<br>Section 10.2.5.2 Track Support Requirements, p.105 (printed 10-26): *"The host message should only contain the Account Number, followed by an account delimiter (=), followed by the 4 digit Expiration Date (Storage format – YYMM)."*<br>Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (Chapter 13), p.372 (printed 13-32): *"For a card whose number has been manually entered, the expiration date is in MMYY format"*<br>Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (Chapter 13), p.372 (printed 13-32): *"Note: For all authorizations, merchants must send a value even for expired cards."* |
| Why it is wrong | This purchase must carry the data read from the card (entry mode 05 = chip read), but Account Number and Card Discretionary Block Data are empty, so BUYPASS cannot identify the card. The test case marks the Account Number AWAITING_CLIENT_VALUE: the Interlink Debit card profile was never supplied, and the generator wrote an empty field instead of a value or an explicit placeholder. The Card Discretionary Block Data is marked as an omitted conditional field, although it is needed here. (TC-002887) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: awaiting_client_value` (473 of 473 files) – flags: `AMBIGUOUS_LENGTH`, `AWAITING_CLIENT_VALUE` |
| Certainty | Certain that BUYPASS cannot process the message; the values themselves need the client's card profile (the test case marks the Account Number `AWAITING_CLIENT_VALUE`) |
| Fix in the generator | Fill in the Account Number and Element 12 from the client's test card: the data read from the card for card-read entry modes, Account Number + expiry YYMM on follow-on messages, the expiry for stored credentials. Until the card profile is supplied, write an explicit placeholder (for example `<CLIENT_CARD_PAN>`) and keep the test case blocked – never an empty string. |
| Check before release | every financial request has an Account Number and Element 12 (or an explicit placeholder) |

… and 393 more rows of the same kind. Every row is in [Consolidated_Defect_Sheet.xlsx](./Consolidated_Defect_Sheet.xlsx), sheet *Field findings* (filter *Defect* = D-15).

#### D-15 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002887, step 1)

1. Open [TD-TC-002887.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002887.json#L14) line 14: value `(empty)`.
2. This purchase must carry the data read from the card (entry mode 05 = chip read), but Account Number and Card Discretionary Block Data are empty, so BUYPASS cannot identify the card. The test case marks the Account Number AWAITING_CLIENT_VALUE: the Interlink Debit card profile was never supplied, and the generator wrote an empty field instead of a value or an explicit placeholder. The Card Discretionary Block Data is marked as an omitted conditional field, although it is needed here.
3. Read in ATL105: Element 2 Account Number (Chapter 13), p.364 (printed 13-24): *"a variable length (up to 24 alphanumeric bytes) Account Number"*; Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (Chapter 13), p.371 (printed 13-31): *"If the Account Number is obtained from a Track 2 card reader, instead of being manually keyed, the Account Number and Card Discretionary Block Data should consist of all data read from the card between, but not including, the Start Sentinel and End Sentinel."*; Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data (Chapter 13), p.371 (printed 13-31): *"Account Number is considered the data before the = character, and Card Discretionary Block Data is considered the data after the = character."*
4. Expected: Fill in the data read from the card (entry mode 05 = chip read) from the client's Interlink Debit test card. Until the card profile is supplied, keep the test case blocked and write an explicit placeholder instead of an empty field.
5. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

<a id="d-16"></a>

### D-16 – The debit PIN block (Element 33) is empty

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 33 Encrypted PIN Block Data (Segment 100) |
| Where in the test data | `"Standard Message Data Segment"` → `"EncryptedPINBlockData"` |
| Type | Required data missing (needs client input) |
| Affected | 242 test cases, 305 test data files in 21 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS3 Purchase/Capture – Magnetic Stripe; TS4 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS7 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS10 Authorization Only – Magnetic Stripe; TS11 Authorization Only – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS12 Authorization Only – Chip Fallback; TS13 Authorization Only – Credential on File; TS14 Authorization Only – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device; TS15 Merchandise Return – EMV Contact Chip; TS16 Merchandise Return – EMV Contactless Chip; TS17 Merchandise Return – Magnetic Stripe; TS18 Merchandise Return – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS19 Merchandise Return – Chip Fallback; TS20 Merchandise Return – Credential on File; TS21 Merchandise Return – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 305 of the 305 debit requests that need a PIN (every step except the Purchase Reversal and other follow-on messages) of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `(empty)` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | Element 33 = DUKPT Key Serial Number + PIN block from the client's test PIN and DUKPT key; until they are supplied, mark the test case blocked (needs client input). |
| ATL105 reference | Section 10.2.1.3 PIN Entry, p.96 (printed 10-17): *"All transactions—except the Purchase Reversal transaction—require PIN entry."*<br>Section 10.2.1.3 PIN Entry, p.96 (printed 10-17): *"When processing a debit card transaction, the entry of the customer PIN is required on a DES-compliant entry device, and it must be encrypted using the DUKPT PIN encryption method."*<br>Element 33 Encrypted PIN Block Data (Chapter 13), p.382 (printed 13-42): *"Required for debit and EBT."*<br>Element 33 Encrypted PIN Block Data (Chapter 13), p.382 (printed 13-42): *"For DUKPT PIN encryption, this is a 16- to 20-byte Key Serial Number (KSN) plus a 16-byte PIN block."* |
| Why it is wrong | ATL105 requires PIN entry, DUKPT-encrypted, on every debit transaction except the Purchase Reversal, and Element 33 is required for debit. This purchase (Transaction Type 0) sends no PIN block, although its chip data reports an online PIN (Tag 9F34 = 420300). (TC-002887) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: omitted` (305 of 305 files) – flags: `CONDITIONAL_FIELD_OMITTED` |
| Certainty | Certain; the value needs the client's test PIN and DUKPT key |
| Fix in the generator | Every debit request except the Purchase Reversal and the follow-on messages: Element 33 = DUKPT Key Serial Number + PIN block (Section 10.2.1.3). Until the client supplies the test PIN and DUKPT key, write an explicit placeholder and keep the test case blocked. |
| Check before release | debit requests that are not follow-on messages or Purchase Reversals: Element 33 not empty |

#### D-16 – actual vs expected, field by field

Actual in every row: `(empty)`
<br>Expected in every row: Element 33 = DUKPT Key Serial Number + PIN block from the client's test PIN and DUKPT key; until they are supplied, mark the test case blocked (needs client input).

#### D-16 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002887, step 1)

1. Open [TD-TC-002887.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002887.json#L16) line 16: value `(empty)`.
2. ATL105 requires PIN entry, DUKPT-encrypted, on every debit transaction except the Purchase Reversal, and Element 33 is required for debit. This purchase (Transaction Type 0) sends no PIN block, although its chip data reports an online PIN (Tag 9F34 = 420300).
3. Read in ATL105: Section 10.2.1.3 PIN Entry, p.96 (printed 10-17): *"All transactions—except the Purchase Reversal transaction—require PIN entry."*; Section 10.2.1.3 PIN Entry, p.96 (printed 10-17): *"When processing a debit card transaction, the entry of the customer PIN is required on a DES-compliant entry device, and it must be encrypted using the DUKPT PIN encryption method."*; Element 33 Encrypted PIN Block Data (Chapter 13), p.382 (printed 13-42): *"Required for debit and EBT."*; Element 33 Encrypted PIN Block Data (Chapter 13), p.382 (printed 13-42): *"For DUKPT PIN encryption, this is a 16- to 20-byte Key Serial Number (KSN) plus a 16-byte PIN block."*
4. Expected: Element 33 = DUKPT Key Serial Number + PIN block from the client's test PIN and DUKPT key; until they are supplied, mark the test case blocked (needs client input).
5. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

---

<a id="d-17"></a>

### D-17 – A keyed (manual) entry mode is used for a debit card

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 113 Table 005 (PAN entry mode) (Segment 111) |
| Where in the test data | `"Variable Information Data Segment"` → `"VariableInformationSection"` (Table 005) |
| Type | Entry mode not allowed for the card program |
| Affected | 34 test cases, 43 test data files in 3 scenarios: TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS12 Authorization Only – Chip Fallback; TS19 Merchandise Return – Chip Fallback |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 43 of the 43 debit test data files with a keyed entry mode (01, 79), follow-on steps excluded of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `791` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | Use a card-read entry mode – 80 (chip fallback to swipe) for the fallback scenario – or drop the keyed debit test case. |
| ATL105 reference | Section 10.2.1.4 Manual Entry, p.96 (printed 10-17): *"Manual entry is not allowed on a debit card transaction."*<br>Appendix J. Valid Point-of-Service Entry Mode Codes, p.638 (printed Appendix J-1): *"79 Contact and Contactless chip fallback to manual"* |
| Why it is wrong | Table 005 entry mode '79' = chip fallback to manual: the card number is keyed in. ATL105 does not allow manual entry on a debit card transaction; when the card cannot be read after three attempts, the transaction is ended, so a chip fallback for debit can only swipe the card (entry mode 80). (TC-003005) |
| Certainty | Certain – Section 10.2.1.4; the catalog's own device-only test T11-DEBIT-NO-MANUAL expects the device to end a debit transaction after three failed reads, without offering manual entry |
| Fix in the generator | Debit: no manual entry (Section 10.2.1.4). For the chip-fallback scenario use entry mode 80 (fallback to swipe), or drop the keyed debit test cases. |
| Check before release | debit requests: Table 005 entry mode is not 01 or 79 |

#### D-17 – actual vs expected, field by field

Actual in every row: `791`
<br>Expected in every row: Use a card-read entry mode – 80 (chip fallback to swipe) for the fallback scenario – or drop the keyed debit test case.

#### D-17 – how to validate manually (example: TC-003005, step 1)

1. Open [TD-TC-003005.json](../test_data/TD-TC-003005.json#L34) line 34: value `791`.
2. Table 005 entry mode '79' = chip fallback to manual: the card number is keyed in. ATL105 does not allow manual entry on a debit card transaction; when the card cannot be read after three attempts, the transaction is ended, so a chip fallback for debit can only swipe the card (entry mode 80).
3. Read in ATL105: Section 10.2.1.4 Manual Entry, p.96 (printed 10-17): *"Manual entry is not allowed on a debit card transaction."*; Appendix J. Valid Point-of-Service Entry Mode Codes, p.638 (printed Appendix J-1): *"79 Contact and Contactless chip fallback to manual"*
4. Expected: Use a card-read entry mode – 80 (chip fallback to swipe) for the fallback scenario – or drop the keyed debit test case.
5. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

<a id="d-19"></a>

### D-19 – Totals Request: Card Label (Element 13) is not 4 characters

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 13 Card Label (Totals Request) (Segment 105) |
| Where in the test data | `"Totals Data Segment"` → `"CardLabel"` |
| Type | Wrong field width |
| Affected | 63 test cases, 63 test data files in 14 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS3 Purchase/Capture – Magnetic Stripe; TS4 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS7 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS10 Authorization Only – Magnetic Stripe; TS11 Authorization Only – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS12 Authorization Only – Chip Fallback; TS13 Authorization Only – Credential on File; TS14 Authorization Only – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 63 of the 63 Totals Request files of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `'DB'` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | 'DB  ' (DB followed by 2 spaces) |
| ATL105 reference | Element 13 Card Label (Chapter 13), p.372 (printed 13-32): *"Fixed length of four alphanumeric characters; left-aligned and space-filled"*<br>Element 13 Card Label (Chapter 13), p.373 (printed 13-33): *"Debit DB"* |
| Why it is wrong | Element 13 is a fixed field of four characters, left-aligned and space-filled. 'DB' has 2, so the fields after it shift by 2 on the wire. (TC-002893) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: spec_rule_value` (63 of 63 files) – flags: `CARD_LABEL` |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Card Label is a fixed 4-character field, left-aligned and space-filled: `DB` → `DB  ` (two trailing spaces). Recalculate the Segment Length afterwards (D-20). |
| Check before release | Card Label has exactly 4 characters |

Actual in every row: `'DB'`
<br>Expected in every row: 'DB  ' (DB followed by 2 spaces)

#### D-19 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002893, step 2)

1. Open [TD-TC-002893.step2.totals.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002893.step2.totals.json#L20) line 20: value `'DB'`.
2. Element 13 is a fixed field of four characters, left-aligned and space-filled. 'DB' has 2, so the fields after it shift by 2 on the wire.
3. Read in ATL105: Element 13 Card Label (Chapter 13), p.372 (printed 13-32): *"Fixed length of four alphanumeric characters; left-aligned and space-filled"*; Element 13 Card Label (Chapter 13), p.373 (printed 13-33): *"Debit DB"*
4. Expected: 'DB  ' (DB followed by 2 spaces)
5. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

<a id="d-20"></a>

### D-20 – Totals Request: Segment 105 length is counted with the wrong layout

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 84 Segment Length (Totals Request) (Segment 105) |
| Where in the test data | `"Totals Data Segment"` → `"SegmentLength"` |
| Type | Wrong length |
| Affected | 63 test cases, 63 test data files in 14 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS3 Purchase/Capture – Magnetic Stripe; TS4 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS7 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS10 Authorization Only – Magnetic Stripe; TS11 Authorization Only – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS12 Authorization Only – Chip Fallback; TS13 Authorization Only – Credential on File; TS14 Authorization Only – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device |
| Systemic | Yes – found in 63 of the 63 Totals Request files of the catalog (every one): it comes from a generator rule, not from one bad value |
| Actual (generated) | `108` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | 107 |
| ATL105 reference | Section 12.6 Totals Data Segment, p.235 (printed 12-18): *"Field Nos. 1–14 are separated by Field Separators, with a Field Separator following Field No. 14."*<br>Section 12.6 Totals Data Segment, p.235 (printed 12-18): *"Field Nos. 15–17 are not separated by Field Separators."*<br>Element 13 Card Label (Chapter 13), p.372 (printed 13-32): *"Fixed length of four alphanumeric characters; left-aligned and space-filled"*<br>Element 84 Segment Length (Chapter 13), p.406 (printed 13-66): *"This figure is calculated using the lengths of all data elements in the segment plus all Field Separators/Product Data Field Delimiters related to those elements."* |
| Why it is wrong | Fields 1-14 add up to 76 characters, plus 14 Field Separators = 90. Fields 15-17 (the Category Total Data Section) are not separated: Card Label 4 (fixed width) + Count 5 + Amount 8 = 17. The Segment Length is therefore 107, not '108'. '108' is what you get with a Field Separator after every one of the 17 fields and the 2-character Card Label, as in Segment 100. (TC-002893) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: derived_at_wire_encoding` (63 of 63 files) – flags: `DERIVED_AT_WIRE_ENCODING` |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Segment 105 length = Fields 1–14 + 14 Field Separators + the Category Total Data Section, which has no separators (Card Label 4 + Count 5 + Amount 8 = 17) (Section 12.6). Do not count a separator after Fields 15–17 and use the 4-character Card Label (D-19). |
| Check before release | declared Segment Length = Fields 1–14 + 14 + 17 |

#### D-20 – actual vs expected, field by field

Actual in every row: `108`
<br>Expected in every row: 107

2. Fields 1-14 add up to 76 characters, plus 14 Field Separators = 90. Fields 15-17 (the Category Total Data Section) are not separated: Card Label 4 (fixed width) + Count 5 + Amount 8 = 17. The Segment Length is therefore 107, not '108'. '108' is what you get with a Field Separator after every one of the 17 fields and the 2-character Card Label, as in Segment 100.
3. Read in ATL105: Section 12.6 Totals Data Segment, p.235 (printed 12-18): *"Field Nos. 1–14 are separated by Field Separators, with a Field Separator following Field No. 14."*; Section 12.6 Totals Data Segment, p.235 (printed 12-18): *"Field Nos. 15–17 are not separated by Field Separators."*; Element 13 Card Label (Chapter 13), p.372 (printed 13-32): *"Fixed length of four alphanumeric characters; left-aligned and space-filled"*; Element 84 Segment Length (Chapter 13), p.406 (printed 13-66): *"This figure is calculated using the lengths of all data elements in the segment plus all Field Separators/Product Data Field Delimiters related to those elements."*
4. Expected: 107
5. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

## 4. Scenario-specific defects

<a id="d-14"></a>

### D-14 – Follow-on step (completion, cancellation, reversal): amount data built from an impossible value

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 58 Nonfuel Amount (test case value), Element 84 Segment Length (Segment 100) |
| Where in the test data | test case `steps[i].request` → `"Nonfuel Amount"`; follow-on step `"Standard Message Data Segment"` → `"SegmentLength"` |
| Type | Impossible amount, wrong length |
| Affected | 7 test cases, 7 test data files in 7 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS3 Purchase/Capture – Magnetic Stripe; TS4 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS7 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device |
| Actual (generated) | 8 different values – see the table below (e.g. `37526954`) |
| Expected (ATL105) | per row in the table below (e.g. Reversal amount = the step 1 response Approved Amount without the leading zeros (for example 000001000 -> 1000).) |
| ATL105 reference | Element 58 Nonfuel Amount (Chapter 13), p.393 (printed 13-53): *"A maximum length of 7 bytes is allowed for all other card types."*<br>Section 10.1.2.1 Partial Approval Transaction, p.84 (printed 10-5): *"The amount in a Reversal (Void) request must be the amount approved in the original transaction response when a transaction has been partially approved."*<br>Element 121 Partial Approval Indicator (Chapter 13), p.440 (printed 13-100): *"the network can approve an amount that is less than the amount in the Authorization request"*<br>Element 6 Approved Amount (Chapter 13), p.368 (printed 13-28): *"Fixed length of nine digits with two assumed decimal places Zero-filled and right aligned"*<br>Section 12.1 Standard Message Data Segment, p.223 (printed 12-6): *"Identifies the data segment's length, including Segment Type's length and Field Separators."*<br>Element 84 Segment Length (Chapter 13), p.406 (printed 13-66): *"This figure is calculated using the lengths of all data elements in the segment plus all Field Separators/Product Data Field Delimiters related to those elements."* |
| Why it is wrong | '37526954' ($375,269.54) has 8 digits. Interlink amounts allow at most 7 digits; 8 digits are allowed for American Express cards only. (TC-002915) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: flow_response_runtime_value` (7 of 7 files) – flags: `FLOW_LINK_NEEDS_RUNTIME_VALUE`, `FLOW_RESPONSE_SHAPE_FILLED_VIA` |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Follow-on step after a partial approval: keep the amount a run-time value from the original response (Approved Amount); any provisional value at most 7 digits and not more than the original amount; Segment Length calculated after substitution. |
| Check before release | provisional amount ≤ 7 digits and ≤ original amount; Segment Length recalculated |

#### D-14 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002915, step 2)

1. Open the step 1 file [TD-TC-002915.step1.purchase.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002915.step1.purchase.json#L19) line 19: `NonfuelAmount` = `1500` ($15.00); the test case expects Response Code `F` (partial approval).
2. Open the step 2 file [TD-TC-002915.step2.reversal.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002915.step2.reversal.json#L19) line 19: `NonfuelAmount` = `<NonfuelAmount from step 1 response>` – a run-time value, which is the correct approach.
3. The test case value for Nonfuel Amount is too long: [test_cases.jsonl line 29](../test_cases/test_cases.jsonl#L29) → `steps[1].request` → `Nonfuel Amount` (used for TD-TC-002915.step2.reversal.json) = `37526954`. '37526954' ($375,269.54) has 8 digits. Interlink amounts allow at most 7 digits; 8 digits are allowed for American Express cards only.
4. The reversal amount cannot be larger than the original sale: [test_cases.jsonl line 29](../test_cases/test_cases.jsonl#L29) → `steps[1].request` → `Nonfuel Amount` (used for TD-TC-002915.step2.reversal.json) = `37526954`. The reversal must carry exactly the amount approved in step 1. A partial approval is always less than the $15.00 requested, so '37526954' ($375,269.54) is impossible.
5. Segment Length was calculated from the invalid test case value: [TD-TC-002915.step2.reversal.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002915.step2.reversal.json#L7) line 7 = `068`. '068' is the length you get with the test case value(s) Nonfuel Amount '37526954', Approval Number 'KPC4BY'. With real values (amount 1 to 4 digits; Approval Number always 6 characters) the Segment Length can only be 061 to 064, so '068' can never be correct.
6. Read in ATL105: Element 58 Nonfuel Amount (Chapter 13), p.393 (printed 13-53): *"A maximum length of 7 bytes is allowed for all other card types."*; Section 10.1.2.1 Partial Approval Transaction, p.84 (printed 10-5): *"The amount in a Reversal (Void) request must be the amount approved in the original transaction response when a transaction has been partially approved."*; Element 121 Partial Approval Indicator (Chapter 13), p.440 (printed 13-100): *"the network can approve an amount that is less than the amount in the Authorization request"*; Element 6 Approved Amount (Chapter 13), p.368 (printed 13-28): *"Fixed length of nine digits with two assumed decimal places Zero-filled and right aligned"*; Section 12.1 Standard Message Data Segment, p.223 (printed 12-6): *"Identifies the data segment's length, including Segment Type's length and Field Separators."*; Element 84 Segment Length (Chapter 13), p.406 (printed 13-66): *"This figure is calculated using the lengths of all data elements in the segment plus all Field Separators/Product Data Field Delimiters related to those elements."*
7. Conclusion: **FAIL** – expected: Reversal amount = the step 1 response Approved Amount without the leading zeros (for example 000001000 -> 1000).; Recalculate the Segment Length after filling in the real values (for example Approved Amount 000001000 -> amount 1000 plus a 6-character Approval Number -> Segment Length 064).
8. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

### D-18 – Cash back: the chip amounts (Tags 9F02, 9F03, 9C) leave out the cash back

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Element 190 EMV Chip Data – Tags 9F02, 9F03 and 9C (Segment 130) |
| Where in the test data | `"EMV Request Data Segment"` → `"EMVChipData"` (Tags 9F02, 9F03, 9C) |
| Type | Chip data does not match the request |
| Affected | 4 test cases, 4 test data files in 2 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip |
| Actual (generated) | `9F02=000000001500, 9F03=000000000000, 9C=00` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | Generate the chip data from a purchase with cash back: 9F02 = 000000002000, 9F03 = 000000000500, 9C = 09. |
| ATL105 reference | Element 17 Cash Amount (Chapter 13), p.375 (printed 13-35): *"Identifies the cash back amount of a transaction."*<br>Element 58 Nonfuel Amount (Chapter 13), p.393 (printed 13-53): *"Identifies the transaction’s dollar amount that is not a fuel, tax, or cash amount."*<br>Appendix R. EMV Chip Data Example, p.697 (printed Appendix R-1): *"9F\02\06\00\00\00\00\09\00\ Amount, authorized (numeric)"*<br>Element 190 EMV Chip Data (Chapter 13), p.478 (printed 13-138): *"All data elements must contain the exact values that were present on the card to terminal interface."* |
| Why it is wrong | This purchase sends a Cash Amount of 500 ($5.00) next to the Nonfuel Amount, which excludes cash. The chip data must hold the exact card-to-terminal values of a cash-back purchase (EMV Book 3: 9F02 = total including the cash back, 9F03 = the cash back, 9C = 09). Here: Tag 9F02 (Amount, Authorized) = 000000001500 = $15.00, but the transaction total including the cash back is $20.00 (000000002000); Tag 9F03 (Amount, Other – the cash back) = 000000000000, but the Cash Amount is 500 (000000000500); Tag 9C (Transaction Type) = 00 (purchase), but a purchase with cash back is 09. (TC-002913) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: spec_tlv_composite` (4 of 4 files) – flags: `EMV_CHIP_DATA_PER_TEST_CASE` |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Purchase with cash back: generate the chip data from a cash-back transaction – 9F02 = Nonfuel Amount + Cash Amount, 9F03 = Cash Amount, 9C = `09` (EMV Book 3) – and recalculate Elements 189 and 84 of Segment 130. |
| Check before release | Cash Amount > 0 → 9F02 = total, 9F03 = Cash Amount, 9C = 09 |

#### D-18 – actual vs expected, field by field

Actual in every row: `9F02=000000001500, 9F03=000000000000, 9C=00`
<br>Expected in every row: Generate the chip data from a purchase with cash back: 9F02 = 000000002000, 9F03 = 000000000500, 9C = 09.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip | [TC-002913](./Purchase_Capture/EMV_Contact_Chip/TC-002913_ATL105_Field_Validation.md) | 1 (purchase) | [TD-TC-002913.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002913.json#L49) line 49 | The chip data does not include the cash back |
| 2 | TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip | [TC-002914](./Purchase_Capture/EMV_Contact_Chip/TC-002914_ATL105_Field_Validation.md) | 1 (purchase) | [TD-TC-002914.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002914.json#L49) line 49 | The chip data does not include the cash back |

EMV Chip Data Example, p.697 (printed Appendix R-1): *"9F\02\06\00\00\00\00\09\00\ Amount, authorized (numeric)"*; Element 190 EMV Chip Data (Chapter 13), p.478 (printed 13-138): *"All data elements must contain the exact values that were present on the card to terminal interface."*
4. Expected: Generate the chip data from a purchase with cash back: 9F02 = 000000002000, 9F03 = 000000000500, 9C = 09.

### D-21 – Totals Request: the totals do not match the transactions of the flow

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Elements 16, 15, 42 Card Type Total Count, Card Type Total Amount, Grand Total (Segment 105) |
| Where in the test data | `"Totals Data Segment"` → `"CardTypeTotalCount"`, `"CardTypeTotalAmount"`, `"GrandTotal"` |
| Type | Totals do not match the flow |
| Affected | 14 test cases, 14 test data files in 14 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS3 Purchase/Capture – Magnetic Stripe; TS4 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS7 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device; TS8 Authorization Only – EMV Contact Chip; TS9 Authorization Only – EMV Contactless Chip; TS10 Authorization Only – Magnetic Stripe; TS11 Authorization Only – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS12 Authorization Only – Chip Fallback; TS13 Authorization Only – Credential on File; TS14 Authorization Only – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device |
| Actual (generated) | `count 00001, amount 00000000, grand total 00000000` in every affected file |
| Expected (ATL105) | per row in the table below (e.g. Leave out the DB bucket and send Grand Total 00000000) |
| ATL105 reference | Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"A Purchase/Capture increases the count and dollar amount of the corresponding category as well as the grand total."*<br>Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"A Reversal decreases the count and dollar amount of the corresponding category as well as the grand total."*<br>Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"A Merchandise Return increases the count and decreases the dollar amount for the corresponding category as well as the grand total."*<br>Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"The grand total includes all transactions except the non-financial transactions grouped in the following categories: • AO—Authorization only"*<br>Section 12.6 Totals Data Segment, p.237 (printed 12-20): *"Field Nos. 15–17 are sent up to a maximum of 18 times—once for each of the following card types—in the order shown and only when data occurs for a card type."* |
| Why it is wrong | Section 9.2 counts each approved transaction into its card bucket: step 1 purchase $15.00: no response (timed out), so not in the totals; step 2 timeout reversal $15.00: its original was never approved, so nothing to take out. The DB bucket nets to count 0 and amount 0 (so it is not sent at all), but the file has count 00001 and amount 00000000. (TC-002896) |
| How the generator produced it | `method: qe_example_value` (14 of 14 files) |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Compute the device totals from the steps before the Totals Request (Section 9.2): approved Purchase/Capture or Completion +1 / +amount; approved Reversal −1 / −amount; Merchandise Return +1 / −amount; Authorization Only into the AO bucket, which the grand total leaves out. A purchase that never got a response and was reversed is not in the totals. A card bucket whose net count is zero is not sent. |
| Check before release | count, amount and grand total = the sums over the approved steps before the Totals Request |

#### D-21 – how to validate manually (example: TC-002896, step 3)

1. Open [TD-TC-002896.step3.totals.json](../test_data/TD-TC-002896.step3.totals.json#L21) line 21: value `count 00001, amount 00000000, grand total 00000000`.
2. Section 9.2 counts each approved transaction into its card bucket: step 1 purchase $15.00: no response (timed out), so not in the totals; step 2 timeout reversal $15.00: its original was never approved, so nothing to take out. The DB bucket nets to count 0 and amount 0 (so it is not sent at all), but the file has count 00001 and amount 00000000.
3. Read in ATL105: Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"A Purchase/Capture increases the count and dollar amount of the corresponding category as well as the grand total."*; Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"A Reversal decreases the count and dollar amount of the corresponding category as well as the grand total."*; Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"A Merchandise Return increases the count and decreases the dollar amount for the corresponding category as well as the grand total."*; Section 9.2 Local Totals, p.72 (printed 9-8): *"The grand total includes all transactions except the non-financial transactions grouped in the following categories: • AO—Authorization only"*; Section 12.6 Totals Data Segment, p.237 (printed 12-20): *"Field Nos. 15–17 are sent up to a maximum of 18 times—once for each of the following card types—in the order shown and only when data occurs for a card type."*
4. Expected: Leave out the DB bucket and send Grand Total 00000000
5. Repeat for the other rows of the table above – only the file, the line and the values change.

Your conclusion: ☐ Agree ☐ Disagree – note: ____________

<a id="d-24"></a>

### D-24 – A test data value differs from the test case it was generated from

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Field | Any field – test data value vs test case request value (Segment 100 / 105) |
| Where in the test data | the field named in each row |
| Type | Generation fidelity |
| Affected | 14 test cases, 14 test data files in 7 scenarios: TS1 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contact Chip; TS2 Purchase/Capture – EMV Contactless Chip; TS3 Purchase/Capture – Magnetic Stripe; TS4 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Magnetic Stripe; TS5 Purchase/Capture – Chip Fallback; TS6 Purchase/Capture – Credential on File; TS7 Purchase/Capture – Contactless Mobile Commerce Device |
| Actual (generated) | 14 different values – see the table below (e.g. `029062`) |
| Expected (ATL105) | per row in the table below (e.g. '029063' (regenerate the file from the test case)) |
| ATL105 reference | Element 86 Sequence Number (Chapter 13), p.409 (printed 13-69): *"Valid Codes/Values: 000001–999999"*<br>Element 86 Sequence Number (Chapter 13), p.409 (printed 13-69): *"Each transaction has a unique Sequence Number."* |
| Why it is wrong | The test data file has '029062', but the test case request (the source the file is generated from) has '029063'. The two are out of step, so the file does not send what the test case describes. '029062' is already the Sequence Number of step 2 (totals) on the same terminal, so the terminal would send one Sequence Number twice. (TC-002906) |
| Certainty | Certain |
| Fix in the generator | Write the test data file from the test case request it belongs to (same values); regenerate the files after any change to the test case. |
| Check before release | every test data value = the test case request value (except run-time placeholders) |

## 5. What passes

Aggregated over all 536 test data files.

| Area | Correct in every test data file | Not correct in every file (files with FAIL / BLOCKED / REVIEW) |
|---|---|---|
| Message / Data Section 1 | Message Format Version Identifier; Number of Segments | Message type (6 REVIEW of 536) |
| Segment 100 | Segment Type; Information Byte; Terminal Identifier; Prompt Code; Pump/Lane Number; Fuel Purchase Amount; Tax Amount; Cash Amount; Local Date and Local Time; Partial Approval Indicator | Segment Length (7 FAIL of 473); Account Number (473 FAIL of 473); Card Discretionary Block Data (473 FAIL of 473); Encrypted PIN Block Data (305 FAIL of 473); Nonfuel Amount (7 BLOCKED of 473); Sequence Number (14 FAIL of 473); Approval Number (77 BLOCKED of 473) |
| Segment 111 | Segment Type; Segment Length; Variable Information Indicator; Variable Information Length; Variable Information (Table 031) | Variable Information (Table 005) (43 FAIL of 473) |
| Segment 130 and EMV chip data | Segment Type; Segment Length; CA Public Key File Checksum; EMV Chip Data Length; EMV Chip Data; Tag 5F2A Transaction Currency Code; Tag 9F1A Terminal Country Code; Tag 9F27 Cryptogram Information Data; Tag 9F34 CVM Results; EMV Additional Information Indicator; EMV Additional Information Length | EMV Card Sequence Number (90 FAIL of 90); Tag 9F02 Amount, Authorized (4 FAIL of 90); Tag 9F03 Amount, Other (4 FAIL of 90); Tag 9C Transaction Type (4 FAIL of 88); EMV Additional Information (90 FAIL of 90) |
| Segment 105 (Totals Request) | Segment Type; Information Byte; Terminal Identifier; Prompt Code; Employee Number; Password; Totals Date; Hardware Version; Software Version; Firmware Version; Sequence Number; Currency Code; Grand Total; Card Type Total Amount | Segment Length (63 FAIL of 63); Card Label (63 FAIL of 63); Card Type Total Count (7 REVIEW of 63) |
| Step-to-step links | 728 of 742 checks PASS | 14 FAIL |
| Expected response codes | 347 of 347 stated codes PASS | open (`?`) codes are listed in §6 |
| TC-declared `rule_checks` | 2626 of 2626 hold | – |
| Spec quotes in the test cases | 1009 of 1287 found word-for-word on the cited page | citation items in §6 |

The rows count the field-format checks. Objective defects (D-03, D-04, D-07, D-10, D-11) are judged against the purpose of the test, so they are listed in §3–§4 even where the field format itself passes.

## 6. Open questions / review items (not defects)

Consolidated from §6 of the findings reports. These do not make a test case FAIL, but each needs an answer or a correction before execution.

| ID | Item | Why it is open | Test cases | Scenarios | Where in ATL105 | Needed from |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-01 | Exact corrected value of Element 192 / framing of Element 118 for indicator 001 (affects D-02) | Appendix T (p.701) lists *"Table ID n3 Fixed value: 001"* and *"Table Length n Fixed"* but never states the Table Length width: `EMVYES` alone (length `006`) or framed with Table ID + Table Length. | 72 | TS1, TS2, TS8, TS9, TS15, TS16 | Appendix T, PDF p.701 (printed T-1) | BUYPASS / SME or a certified device trace |
| Q-02 | The CA Public Key File Checksum is a sample value | The format is right (25 digits), but the value must be the checksum received in the device's last CA Public Key File Load; the test case marks it as a configured test-data value. (example: TC-002887) | 72 | TS1, TS2, TS8, TS9, TS15, TS16 | Element 187, PDF p.477 (printed 13-137) | Host CA Public Key file |
| Q-03 | Cryptogram, ATC, unpredictable number, TVR and IAD are generated placeholders | ATL105 requires the exact values from the card-to-terminal interface; generated values cannot be authenticated by the issuer. (example: TC-002887) | 72 | TS1, TS2, TS8, TS9, TS15, TS16 | Element 190, PDF p.478 (printed 13-138) | Card simulator / HSM |
| Q-04 | Outdated page cited for the POS Entry Mode table (Table 005) | The test case cites p.548 for the POS Entry Mode table (Table 005). In ATL105 2026-4, PDF p.548 (printed Appendix H-2) does not contain it; it is on PDF p.555 (printed Appendix I-6). p.548 is where it was in the older 2026-3 edition. (example: TC-002887) | 249 | all | Appendix I, PDF p.555 (printed I-6) | Test case generator (citation) |
| Q-05 | The quote cited for p.512 is not word-for-word in ATL105 | The test case quotes "48 Interlink - Authorizer Type: Debit" for p.512. Not word-for-word: all its words are on the cited page, but not in this form. (example: TC-002887) | 278 | all | – | Test case generator (citation) |
| Q-06 | The Terminal Identifier is a placeholder | The test case flags the merchant as a placeholder (MERCHANT_PLACEHOLDER), so 'JX72948216050257001' was generated, not assigned by BUYPASS. Its State Code (positions 3-4) '72' = Yukon (YT), Canada. (example: TC-002887) | 249 | all | Element 102, PDF p.417 (printed 13-77); Appendix D, PDF p.517 (printed D-4) | Client merchant list / BUYPASS |
| Q-07 | The device version fields of the Totals Request are generated values | ATL105 requires the device's hardware, software and firmware versions on a Totals Request. The test case generated random values, so they do not identify a certified device. (example: TC-002893) | 63 | TS1, TS2, TS3, TS4, TS5, TS6, TS7, TS8, TS9, TS10, TS11, TS12, TS13, TS14 | Element 43, PDF p.386 (printed 13-46); Element 96, PDF p.414 (printed 13-74); Element 39, PDF p.384 (printed 13-44) | Client (versions of the certified device) |

Element 83 has a separate approval code for each kind of transaction. (example: TC-002893) | 91 | all | Element 83, PDF p.404 (printed 13-64) | Test case owner |
| Q-09 | The totals bucket is sent with a count of 00000 | Element 16 allows 00001-99999, and the category section is sent only when data occurs for a card type. A net count of 00000 suggests the bucket should not be sent at all. (example: TC-002894) | 7 | TS1, TS2, TS3, TS4, TS5, TS6, TS7 | Element 16, PDF p.375 (printed 13-35); Section 12.6, PDF p.237 (printed 12-20) | BUYPASS / SME |

## 7. Recommendations for the AI test data generator (field by field)

| # | Field | What to change | Defect | Check before release |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Element 2 Account Number and Element 12 Card Discretionary Block Data | Fill in the Account Number and Element 12 from the client's test card: the data read from the card for card-read entry modes, Account Number + expiry YYMM on follow-on messages, the expiry for stored credentials. Until the card profile is supplied, write an explicit placeholder (for example `<CLIENT_CARD_PAN>`) and keep the test case blocked – never an empty string. | [D-15](#d-15) | every financial request has an Account Number and Element 12 (or an explicit placeholder) |
| 2 | Element 33 Encrypted PIN Block Data | Every debit request except the Purchase Reversal and the follow-on messages: Element 33 = DUKPT Key Serial Number + PIN block (Section 10.2.1.3). Until the client supplies the test PIN and DUKPT key, write an explicit placeholder and keep the test case blocked. | [D-16](#d-16) | debit requests that are not follow-on messages or Purchase Reversals: Element 33 not empty |
| 3 | Element 58 Nonfuel Amount (test case value), Element 84 Segment Length | Follow-on step after a partial approval: keep the amount a run-time value from the original response (Approved Amount); any provisional value at most 7 digits and not more than the original amount; Segment Length calculated after substitution. | [D-14](#d-14) | provisional amount ≤ 7 digits and ≤ original amount; Segment Length recalculated |
| 4 | Any field – test data value vs test case request value | Write the test data file from the test case request it belongs to (same values); regenerate the files after any change to the test case. | [D-24](#d-24) | every test data value = the test case request value (except run-time placeholders) |
| 5 | Element 113 Table 005 (PAN entry mode) | Debit: no manual entry (Section 10.2.1.4). For the chip-fallback scenario use entry mode 80 (fallback to swipe), or drop the keyed debit test cases. | [D-17](#d-17) | debit requests: Table 005 entry mode is not 01 or 79 |
| 6 | Element 113 Tables 032 / 049 / 056 (Credential on File indicators) | Credential on File: add Table 032 sub-table 08 (`C`; `I` on the transaction that stores the credential), Table 049 sub-table 02 (Transaction Initiation Indicator `C` or `M`) and, for MasterCard, Table 056 sub-table 09 (MIT/CIT category) on every COF step, including reversals and completions. | [D-09](#d-09) | every COF step has Tables 032-08 and 049-02 (and 056-09 for MasterCard); the storing step has 032-08 = `I` |
| 7 | Element 113 Table 030 (POS Condition Code) | Add Table 030 (POS Condition Code, 10 digits, values from Appendix Q for the certified terminal) to every EMV request and every Credential on File request. | [D-12](#d-12) | Segment 130 present or entry mode 10 → Table 030 present |
| 8 | Element 118 EMV Additional Information (with Elements 191/192) | Indicator `001`: send EMV Table Data `EMVYES` (device can receive EMV table data) or `EMVNOT` and set Element 192 to its length. Apply the Appendix T values instead of filling the 984-character maximum, never reuse a generated string across test cases, and recalculate the Segment 130 length. | [D-02](#d-02) | Element 118 is `EMVYES` or `EMVNOT` (or the confirmed framed form) and Element 192 = its length |
| 9 | Element 188 EMV Card Sequence Number | Element 188 = Tag 5F34 right-aligned and zero-padded to 3 digits (`01` → `001`); 3 spaces when Tag 5F34 is absent. Recalculate the Segment 130 Segment Length afterwards. | [D-01](#d-01) | Element 188 has 3 characters and equals Tag 5F34 zero-padded |
| 10 | Element 190 EMV Chip Data – Tags 9F02, 9F03 and 9C | Purchase with cash back: generate the chip data from a cash-back transaction – 9F02 = Nonfuel Amount + Cash Amount, 9F03 = Cash Amount, 9C = `09` (EMV Book 3) – and recalculate Elements 189 and 84 of Segment 130. | [D-18](#d-18) | Cash Amount > 0 → 9F02 = total, 9F03 = Cash Amount, 9C = 09 |
| 11 | Element 13 Card Label (Totals Request) | Card Label is a fixed 4-character field, left-aligned and space-filled: `DB` → `DB  ` (two trailing spaces). Recalculate the Segment Length afterwards (D-20). | [D-19](#d-19) | Card Label has exactly 4 characters |
| 12 | Element 84 Segment Length (Totals Request) | Segment 105 length = Fields 1–14 + 14 Field Separators + the Category Total Data Section, which has no separators (Card Label 4 + Count 5 + Amount 8 = 17) (Section 12.6). Do not count a separator after Fields 15–17 and use the 4-character Card Label (D-19). | [D-20](#d-20) | declared Segment Length = Fields 1–14 + 14 + 17 |
| 13 | Elements 16, 15, 42 Card Type Total Count, Card Type Total Amount, Grand Total | Compute the device totals from the steps before the Totals Request (Section 9.2): approved Purchase/Capture or Completion +1 / +amount; approved Reversal −1 / −amount; Merchandise Return +1 / −amount; Authorization Only into the AO bucket, which the grand total leaves out. A purchase that never got a response and was reversed is not in the totals. A card bucket whose net count is zero is not sent. | [D-21](#d-21) | count, amount and grand total = the sums over the approved steps before the Totals Request |
| 14 | All Segment Length fields | Recalculate every Segment Length after the fixes above (Segment 100: 17 fields + 17 Field Separators; Segment 130: as in Section 12.20; Segment 105: Fields 1–14 + 14 Field Separators + Card Label, Count and Amount without separators). | D-01, D-02, D-14, D-19, D-20 | declared length = recalculated length |
| 15 | ATL105 citations in the test cases | Cite the 2026-4 pages and the section that matches the transaction (citation items in §6). The data values are not affected. | – | every quote found word-for-word on the cited page |
| 16 | Element 102 Terminal Identifier | Replace the placeholder Terminal Identifier with the BUYPASS-assigned one before execution (§6). | – | Terminal Identifier from the client merchant list |
